# Binary recorded extractor and C3 (2026-10-21)

Branch `codex/cx-extractor-bin-20261021`, base `0e686c0a7`.
New modules only; existing Lean and production sources remain unchanged.

## Stage 1: extractor

`RawCutWordsFromRunExecutableBin` recovers three maps using
`BinCanonicalCutWords`: eighteen Fin 2 slots, strict binary node recovery,
unchanged frame replay, root offsets, typed projections and GS byte extractor.
Its `wordsFromRun_eq_buildWords` proves equality with the binary compiler
builder under the existing valid-prefix contract. Recovery composes the
existing BinMerkleRecovery/BinRawByteCutRecovery stack.

`RawRecordedPublicSpendExtractorBin.returned_relation` proves every returned
witness satisfies `LiteralProtocol.relation` for the run's public bytes
(parsed public `Rspend`, including AppendBinding). No acceptance is required.
`extract_of_checks_noHit_bank` proves completeness for terminal Checks on
the transcript enriched with these binary words, absence of classifier hits,
and success of the actual private byte bank. All witness checking, semantic
public parsing, enriched-field identity and terminal extraction are reused.

Focused receipts are under `evidence/extractor-bin-20261021/manifest.json`.
Seven targets passed; all reported axioms are standard. The first draft of
the frame bridge needed a final definitional equality; its failed receipt is
retained and excluded from accepted results. No package replay was run.

## Stage 2: binary C3

The model experiment now compiles with
`BinCompiledRunWordStability.wordsFromRun D`. The actual extractor uses
`RawCutRecordedWordsExecutableBin.wordsFromRecordedCutsExec`; its equality
with BinCanonicalCutWords is proved, and `BinSharedGrammarBridge` connects
the strict node/leaf encodings to SharedBinaryRecordGrammar. The executable
first-index recursion is reused, not copied.

`LiteralTableWordChoiceBin.choose` uses binary table recovery with the same
causal root parsers. `RawRecordedOracleExtractionMassBin.failureMass` counts
failures of the binary extractor, including parse/default/bank branches.
`RawChecksAdaptiveCleanModelBin.extractor_word_alignment` proves total word
alignment and a reannotation fixed point using the binary M7 stability
result and the constructor schedule forced by Checks. Its clean-model
consumer and `RawChecksAdaptiveFailureNoModelBin` instantiate the M9
five-reader mass directly. No old/binary parser equality is assumed.

`R1FinalSoundnessBin.final_soundness` derives Checks from the original named
source laws, derives `h_R12_binary_words : BinaryClean` locally, and composes
the binary failure bound with the single query-scan charge. `h_Rwire` now
concerns the binary compiler AND binary table chooser; the historical M9
completion hypothesis cannot be silently reused. It remains an explicit
source/decoder obligation. All source-row laws refer to the binary compiled
rows via DecoderInstanceBoundaryBin, with unchanged hypothesis names.

The per-attempt bound is unchanged numerically:

```
6 (Q+1161)^2 / 2^256 + 33*15/2^109 + 2^-126 + 2^-454.
```

`R1FinalSoundnessNumericBin.numeric_iff` reuses the proved exact rational
endpoint; `numeric-endpoint.json` independently recomputes it using Fraction
and integer square root. Largest adversary Q:
**22482956455467539568187**; largest total first reads:
**22482956455467539569348**. The next integer fails the strict inequality.
This is maximality of the conservative bound, not a lower bound on actual
failure. The 33 recognized starts remain a separate restriction.

## Stage 3: M10 statement fix

`RawExtractorCostComposedBin.LedgerRefinement e p Q` is the repaired
contract. Its `bounded_jobs`, `reads`, and `bit_accounting` fields quantify
only over accepted-tagged runs with **run.1.length ≤ Q+1161**. The additional
`log_capacity` field requires Q+1161 ≤ p.logSize. Parameters, numerical
budgets, Jobs and the arithmetic-charge lemmas are reused unchanged.
`accepted_attempt_cost` proves both deterministic and private-tape mean
bounds on this domain. Abort tapes are included.

`queryPolynomial a k c` is explicitly
`budgetPolynomial a k` composed with `X+C c`. Its evaluation equals the
diagonal attempt budget at Q+c. `accepted_attempt_polynomial` and
`R1FinalSoundnessBin.uniform_cost` prove the uniform family bound in Q
when one fixed overhead pair a,k and coordinate slack c bound the actual
implementation family. These are conditional implementation theorems.
They do not choose a,k after inspecting each run.

`RawRecordedExtractorBranchTraceBin.value` follows the actual binary
parser/bank/candidate branches. `attachResources` supplies exact output
refinement for any proposed resource implementation; generic Option/trace
lemmas from the old branch module are reused. The three-call event bound
is NOT a bound on work inside those calls.

The final C3 theorem now includes `e`, `p`, the repaired `h_M10_ledger` and
`h_M10_log_length`. It concludes accepted-run mean cost ≤p.attemptBudget,
returned-witness validity, the combined abort allowance, the binary deployed
failure bound and its strict numeric endpoint. Value refinement is supplied
by the branch constructor when it is used; it is never inferred from labels.

### Exact obstruction after the statement fix

`RawRecordedLogLengthBin.repeated_first_reads` proves symbolically that n+1
copies of one byte query have exactly one first read. Consequently
`first_read_budget_does_not_bound_log` constructs accepted-tagged RunViews
with firstReads ≤Q+1161 but length >Q+1161. No huge repeated list is reduced.
`compiled_log_length` also shows the compiler appends the verifier replay,
even though it preserves firstReads. Thus **h_R19 alone does not prove
h_M10_log_length**. The latter must be proved for the chosen actual recorded
execution/representation; any deduplication or removal of the replay would
need an output/cut refinement before using this exact experiment. The
counterexample asserts no frozen-source reachability.

`binary_cut_value` identifies the existing instrumented scan's value with
the binary cut. `bounded_binary_cut_batch` proves 7*n*(Q+1161) for an
explicit list of at most 7*n primitive cut scans. This counts inspected
entries only: not byte comparison/copying, node/leaf table construction,
the eighteen-level lookup walks, frame replay or GS work. It is therefore
not promoted to the full ledger's reads field.

`RawExtractorLedgerBoundaryBin.value_and_size_insufficient` proves that
output equality and even an empty accepted-tagged log do not imply the
repaired ledger for an arbitrary CostedExtractor. It uses deliberately
inflated resource labels as an interface counterexample, never as a cost
implementation. This rules out discharging adequacy from value and size
alone; it does not rule out a genuine instrumented implementation.

**Stopped M10 discharge:** the statement fix is implemented, but the prior
packet contains no complete resource function/job enumeration for
recovery/frame replay, byte parsing/copying, GS/interpolation, RR retries,
filter/merge, final witness checking and representation-bit work. The new
Bin recovery also needs those calls/sizes accounted with eighteen levels.
No theorem supplies those missing implementation facts. Hence neither
h_M10_ledger nor implementation h_M10_uniform is discharged. The uniform
polynomial algebra and its C3 consumer are proved; actual efficiency remains
conditional, rather than assigning free labels or redefining the opaque
external M10_uniform proposition to make it true.

## Verification and release limits

`evidence/extractor-bin-20261021/manifest.json` records every attempt:
source snapshot/hash and revision, exact target/argv/ordered LEAN_PATH,
direct-import hashes/fingerprint, exit, wall time, peak RSS, swaps, complete
axiom lines and logs. The source revision is the checkout commit before each
compile; the exact uncommitted source is pinned by its SHA and snapshot.
`audit.py` checks all final source/object/import hashes, theorem audit coverage,
standard-only axioms and that every tracked file change versus base is an
addition. No shared object was overwritten.

There are 23 new Lean modules, 50 theorems (including private helpers), and
60 accepted axiom reports. Final targets all exit 0, with zero swaps. The
maximum final target RSS is 3,628,908,544 bytes. No memory failure or raised
cap occurred. Commands use the pinned Lean 4.32.0 binary, -j1 -M4400
-DElab.async=false, the shared mkdir lock/one-process gate, and private
`/tmp/codex-cx-extractor-bin-20261021-objects`.

Failures retained: a missing final definitional equality in the replayed-frame
bridge; a missing Table namespace in the binary chooser; missing imports in
the clean consumer; a finite-address cardinality lemma inapplicable to Bytes
(replaced by a symbolic cached-read induction); a missing mean_mono namespace
and polynomial Nat-to-Rat expected-type inference (fixed with explicit cast).
All failed sorryAx error-recovery lines are excluded from accepted audits.

The runtime spelling of binary cut recovery was added after the initial
specification bridge, then composed into the extractor. Only modules with
changed source or direct-import fingerprints were checked again. The driver
refused identical source/dependency inputs, which were skipped rather than
worked around. Successful dependent modules whose object hashes remained
identical need no unchanged repeat. There was no full regression rerun.

This is focused cached-object proof evidence, **not C2/coherent frozen release
replay**, source deployment soundness, or an unconditional efficiency result.
No frozen manifest, Rust/SBF gate, runtime suite, deployment, wallet/account
operation or push was performed. Those suites have no changed covered source
here. All pre-existing Lean and production files are unchanged from base.

Recheck without Lean:

```
python3 -B docs/research/100bit-spend-path-20261013/evidence/extractor-bin-20261021/audit.py
python3 -B docs/research/100bit-spend-path-20261013/evidence/extractor-bin-20261021/numeric.py
```

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): hN is an adversary restriction — claim must be per query

Landed from `codex/cx-hn-starts-20261021` (`design/HN-STARTS-20261021.md`).
`recorded_more_than_33` proves that a fixed adversary querying 34 canonical
initialisation frames for unused public strings, then running any prover P,
has more than 33 recognised starts for every oracle, with acceptance
preserved. So `hN` (≤33 starts) is a restriction on the adversary, not a
protocol fact, and the earlier endpoint "deployedFailure < 2^-100 for
Q ≤ maxAdversaryQueries" was conditional on it. That endpoint must not be
cited without `hN`.

`R1FinalSoundnessBinFirstReadBudget.final_soundness` removes `hN` and derives
starts ≤ Q+1161 from R19, giving
`6(Q+1161)²/2^256 + (Q+1161)·15/2^109 + 2^-126 + 2^-454`. The per-start mass
15/2^109 ≈ 2^-105.09 is the real security parameter; the bound grows linearly
in the number of starts, as any Fiat–Shamir grinding bound does. The 1161
offset makes it exceed 2^-100 even at Q=0 (`replacement_not_100_bits`).

Lead ruling for the next step, pending the user's confirmation of the release
wording: the right form of "100-bit soundness" here is the query-normalised
one, ε(Q) ≤ (Q+1)·2^-100 over a stated query range, which is standard for
Fiat–Shamir arguments. Proving it needs the start count tied to the
adversary's own initialisation-frame queries plus a small constant for the
verifier's and compiler's reads, instead of the R19 total Q+1161. A job is
open for that. Release language must say "per oracle query", not "per attempt",
until the user rules otherwise.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): query-normalised endpoint proved

Landed from `codex/cx-hn-query-20261021` (`design/HN-QUERY-20261021.md`).
`R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQuery.final_soundness` has no `hN`. Its new argument
`hqueries : DecoderQueryAccounting.AdversaryQueries P Q` is the experiment's
query budget (Q bounds the oracle queries the adversary issues), not a
protocol assumption. Starts are bounded symbolically by Q + 34: the honest
verifier contributes at most 34 and compiler replay none
(`BinHonestQueryBudget.recorded_starts`). Conclusion:

    deployedFailure ≤ (Q+34)·B + κ(Q+1161) + 2^-126 + 2^-454
    Q ≤ Qmax → deployedFailure ≤ (Q+1)·2^-100,   Qmax > 2^153

with B = 15/2^109. This is the current headline soundness endpoint, in the
per-query form ruled in the previous addendum. The remaining premises are
exactly those of C3-BIN (R16, R4 row 31, R13, R14, R15, R3, R18, R19, R-wire,
R9 transport, M10 ledger and log length). A sharper initialisation-only
charge, `InitialisationCharging`, is named and proved conditionally but not
used by the endpoint. Seven modules, 29 standard-axiom audits, peak RSS
4.56 GB, zero swap.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): R15 needs the concrete decision map

Landed from `codex/cx-r15-writer-20261021` (`design/R15-WRITER-20261021.md`):
seven model lemmas (checked-opening matching; preservation through W, V,
gamma, alpha0; saved-ten/resumed-twelve composition) and an acceptance-only
counterexample. R15 stays open because the endpoint's decision `d` is
arbitrary and the tx1 finish boundary allows arbitrary checkpoint bytes; P2′
gives same-bytes persistence only. This is the soundness analogue of D85 on

soundness endpoint to the constructed verifier decision map and checkpoint

and R15 at that instance, and leave only Rust-to-model joins.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): R16 constructor erasure; canonical-prover reduction

Landed from `codex/cx-r16-decision-20261021` (`design/R16-DECISION-20261021.md`):
`R16DecisionErasure` proves the decision depends only on the erased
transcript and gives a compiled countermodel, where a prover chooses a row-31
constructor (`.second`/`.c2` instead of `.payload`) that no erased decision
can see, so no `SourceInputs` satisfies R16 for it. `R16DecisionCheckpoint`
builds the typed saved-ten / resumed-twelve writer model with R15 prefix
transport and explicit first-match non-vacuity. R16 and R15 stay premises.

Lead ruling: this is an artefact of a typed message carrier, not an attack,
since the deployed verifier sees bytes. The repair is a reduction, not a
restricted quantifier. For every prover P, the canonical lift
(`CanonicalLiteralProof.liftProver`) has the same oracle-query log, the same
erased transcript and bytes, and so (by erasure congruence) the same
acceptance, output and failure event. Soundness over canonical provers then
implies it over all P, and R16 is provable for canonical provers. Next job:
prove that reduction and a premise-reduced endpoint over arbitrary P.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): R3 — same root cause as R15/R16

Landed from `codex/cx-r3-semantic-20261021` (`design/R3-SEMANTIC-20261021.md`):
all ten semantic polynomial degree guards proved from literal parsing, a
typed semantic-decision acceptance projection, and symbolic R2-chain to

endpoint's byte decision `d` is arbitrary and is not identified with the
typed semantic checker. `R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryR3` keeps R3 verbatim
(its equation-only form is equivalent on parsed transcripts, not weaker).

Lead plan: R3, R13, R14, R15 and R16 all need one concrete verifier decision
instance d₀ — parse, degree guards, semantic HCHECKS, query/opening checks,
saved-ten / resumed-twelve checkpoint — as the Lean model of the frozen
verifier. The endpoint is then specialised to d₀ (with the canonical-prover
reduction for P), and the frozen Rust verifier's equality with d₀ becomes the
one source join, which is what session A's S-cone re-pins establish. That
job starts after the canonical-prover reduction reports, so the two do not
build competing constructions.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): canonical reduction proved; the d₀ job starts

Landed from `codex/cx-r16-canon-20261021` (`design/R16-CANON-20261021.md`):
erasure-preserving retagging, query-log preservation, verifier congruence,
transfer of the extractor's output and returned relation (the recorded
extractor factors through erased messages), equality of failure event and
probability, deployed-transport equivalence, and conditional checkpoint
composition. Total canonical lifting is false: `.initial` at row 31 has no
valid canonical replacement with the same erased transcript, and an
arbitrary d can accept it. So R16 and R15 need d to reject malformed
schedules. This is exactly the concrete-decision plan from the R3 addendum.
The d₀ job now starts: construct d₀ (schedule admission, parse, degree guards,
semantic HCHECKS, query/opening checks, saved-ten / resumed-twelve
checkpoint), compose with the retagging reduction, and discharge R3, R15 and
R16 at d₀, plus R13/R14 where they follow. The frozen-verifier-equals-d₀
equation is left as the single source join.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): d₀ — the decision carrier is too weak; question for the user

Landed from `codex/cx-d0-decision-20261021` (`design/D0-DECISION-20261021.md`):
erased schedule admission with the arbitrary-prover canonical reduction, R16
instance fields on admitted canonical runs, and composition of the typed
semantic, matching, checkpoint and backend checks. Two compiled
obstructions follow.

1. `D0ErasedWordObstruction.no_erased_final_reader`: the endpoint's decision
   `d : ErasedPrefix → ErasedMessage → Bool` is a pure function of the erased
   transcript, while the checked C1/C2/final words are filled in by the
   compiler from the whole oracle log. No erased d₀ can read them.
2. `D0OpeningRecoveryObstruction.depth18_counterexample`: with the zero
   oracle, two different packed leaves both open at one root. Recovering
   leaves from successful openings needs collision exclusion, and
   R13/R15-style premises stated for every H are stronger than needed.

Root cause: in `RawCutProverCompiler.verifier`, which is
`FS2.verifier … (decisionOnBytes d)`, the final decision cannot query the
oracle, so the verifier's Merkle path recomputation is not modelled as
hashing. It is carried by the source premises (R13, R15, R16, R3) instead.

Safe now: narrow each of those premises to non-collision runs. That is a
strict weakening, and the endpoint already charges κ for collisions.
Proposed, pending the user: generalise the decision to an oracle program
(`ErasedPrefix → ErasedMessage → Program Bytes State Bool`). A pure d embeds
with no queries, so existing results should become special cases, and the
verifier's own hash reads are already inside the 561 ≤ 1161 budget. A
feasibility study runs tonight; it changes no existing endpoint.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): five source premises weakened to non-collision runs

Landed from `codex/cx-nocoll-20261021` (`design/NOCOLL-20261021.md`).
`R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryNoColl.final_soundness` adds the antecedent
`¬ binaryCollision (Q + 1161) (eval H (recorded D d P))` to h_R16_instance,
h_R13, h_R14_backend, h_R15_writer and h_R3_semantic, each with a checked
old-to-new implication; the lead diffed the signatures, and those five
insertions are the only change. The conclusion is unchanged. The premises
now ask nothing on runs whose collision mass κ is already charged, which
removes the D0 depth-18 obstruction. h_M10_log_length keeps its form,
because the accepted-run cost conclusion includes colliding runs. Current
headline soundness endpoint:
`R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryNoColl.final_soundness`. 19 standard-axiom
reports, zero swap.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): oracle-decision study — recommend, pending the user

Landed from `codex/cx-oracle-dec-20261021` (`design/ORACLE-DECISION-STUDY-20261021.md`),
in new `OracleDecisionFeasibility*` modules only; no endpoint touched. It
proves an exact pure-decision embedding (programs, logs, acceptance and public
output preserved, so the current results are the special case), oracle-
decision enrichment with compiler replay preserving distinct first reads,
terminal-query budget accounting, and Merkle leaf recovery at any depth on
collision-free runs, also through the compiled experiment. That is the
positive counterpart of the D0 depth-18 obstruction. 28 standard-axiom
theorems, zero swap.

Estimated port: 70–95 modules, 180–280 declarations. Prerequisites: a
sharp full-verifier budget (561 ≤ 1161 alone is not enough), proof that the
decision's terminal queries keep M9's decoder-start bound, and revised M10
log-length accounting. Lead recommendation: adopt it, because it is the
faithful model of a verifier that recomputes Merkle paths, and it lets R13,
R15, R16 and R3 become provable model facts plus one Rust-equals-model join.
The port waits for the user's decision; the three prerequisites (additive
only) run tonight.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-20 night): R-wire tag premise reduced to a decision rejection law

Landed from `codex/cx-inittag-20261021` (`design/INITTAG-20261021.md`).
Tag 2 does not follow from protocol syntax; compiled counterexamples refute
unconditional tag admission, and show that the old premise cannot imply
rejection on unreachable transcripts. `R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryTag`
replaces only `h_Rwire_tag`, using `InitialTagDecision.CheckedRejects`: the
decision rejects any reachable, collision-free, format-checked transcript
whose public tag is not 2. The old premise is proved to imply the new one, and
the new one is proved strictly weaker; the signature diff shows that line
only. The residual is the frozen verifier's S2 header check, which is part of
the verifier-equals-model join. Current headline soundness endpoint:
`R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryTag.final_soundness`. Eleven standard-axiom
audits, zero swap.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 00:00): oracle-decision prerequisites, independent-path version

Landed from `codex/cx-oracle-prereq-20261021` (`design/ORACLE-DECISION-PREREQ-20261021.md`),
new modules only. For a decision that checks every opening's Merkle path
independently: exact decision budget B_V(q,d,f) = 115 + 3q(d+1) + f = 1375
(above 1161); safe recorded decoder starts Q + 1294 (keeping Q + 34 needs
zero recognised terminal starts, and excluding initialisation frames alone
is not enough); recorded log ≤ Q + 2750 (compiled examples refute the old
constant). With κ(Q+1375) the per-query (Q+1)·2^-100 form holds for
39 ≤ Q ≤ Qmax − 466, or for 0 ≤ Q ≤ Qmax − 428 if 34 starts are kept. 51
standard-axiom audits, zero swap.

Lead note: the deployed verifier uses shared binary multiproofs, not
independent paths, so these are upper bounds. A port would model the
multiproof decision and prove the shared-path correspondence, which should
bring back 1161 and Q + 34, plus ledger adequacy for the larger log. That
lowers the port's risk; the decision to port is still the user's.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 00:40): multiproof oracle decision keeps 1161

Landed from `codex/cx-oracle-multi-20261021` (`design/ORACLE-DECISION-MULTI-20261021.md`),
new modules only. For the deployed shared-path multiproof decision: the
verifier call count is B_V_multi = 181 + S + D ≤ 1161, so the existing
constant stands; minimal shared authentication subtrees are built from the
query indices; and collision-free leaf recovery is proved through shared
paths and through the compiled recorded log. A compiled depth-18 example
makes one terminal query a recognised decoder start, so the zero-start
shortcut fails. The conservative bound is Q + 1080 starts (Q + 34 open). The
recorded log is ≤ Q + 2322, and M10 adequacy must be shown on that larger
domain. 13 modules, 81 standard-axiom audits, zero swap.

Lead note: with Q + 1080 starts the per-query form (Q+1)·2^-100 holds for
Q ≥ 32 rather than for all Q. Keeping Q + 34 needs Merkle node inputs never
to parse as initialisation frames, i.e. domain separation between node
hashes and transcript initial frames. Whether the frozen byte formats already
give that (distinct lengths or prefixes) is a cheap question to check before
any port.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 00:50): domain separation is partial

Landed from `codex/cx-domsep-20261021` (`design/DOMAIN-SEPARATION-20261021.md`).
The frozen binary node grammar is `12 ‖ left32 ‖ right32`, the same as Lean.
Frozen nodes and leaves are proved never to equal a public initialisation
frame, and they lie outside the inspected valid source-frame envelope.
However, the current decoder recognises some frozen node inputs as ordinary
semantic absorbs (compiled example `12 ‖ 00^32 ‖ a1 ‖ 00^31`), so universal
node rejection is false, and under an oracle-decision port Q + 1080 stays the
conservative start bound. A row-aware decoder refinement, recognising an
absorb only at its expected transcript row, would need no deployed or CU
change, but needs density and completion proofs. This bears only on the port
and on how tight its bound is; the current headline is unaffected. 17
standard-axiom audits, endpoint hashes unchanged.

Landed from `codex/cx-r17-geometry-20261021` (`design/R17-GEOMETRY-20261021.md`).
Both 512-entry stored geometry tables pass exhaustive byte comparison against

262,144 fibres, 1,048,576 stored slots, line parents and transport maps.
Fourteen Lean theorems prove the closed forms equal the model and give the

`evidence/m10-instr6-20261021/blocked-composition/`. `h_M10_ledger` and

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 01:00): R22 pass 2, and R22 as a corollary of the S-cone refinements

Landed from `codex/cx-r22-overflow2-20261021`: 43 local Kani targets (canonical
decoders, checkpoint reads, concrete backend arithmetic, residue phases, all
64/256-row push positions, composed Merkle group checks) and four Lean
theorems, standard axioms, zero swap. 8,237 census sites stay open
(`evidence/r22-overflow2-20261021/remaining-summary.tsv` groups them by
family); Kani cannot stub the owned-Vec weight reader.

Lead ruling: stop the census route. Aeneas translates Rust integer operations
as checked arithmetic, so an overflow becomes an error result. An S-cone
refinement theorem of total form, ∀ inputs, the extracted function returns
`ok v` with v equal to the model, already proves that no checked operation on
that cone overflows on any input, so the wrapping SBF semantics and the
checked semantics agree there. That is exactly R22. R22 should therefore be
discharged as a corollary of the S1–S10 re-pins (and the P6-route Kani
certificates, where Kani's default overflow checks give the same), provided
each re-pin theorem is stated in total form and not only on accepted inputs.
Next R22 step: once the re-pins land, an audit of which cone theorems are
total and which need a totality lemma. No further census passes.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 01:20): M10 pass 7 — composition done; allocation mirror still free

Landed from `codex/cx-m10-instr7-20261021` (`design/M10-INSTR7-20261021.md`).
The abstract stage interface worked: the complete extractor value equality
and linear work composition are proved through abstract stage laws (peak
3.97 GB, under the cap). The uniform coordinates are proved, so
`h_M10_uniform` is gone. In `R1FinalSoundnessBinPerQueryM10`, `h_M10_ledger`
is replaced by `h_M10_allocation : AllocationBound heap Q`, with the
conditional accepted cost ≤ 4·scale³·(Q+1162)³ (degree 3). 19 modules, 44
standard-axiom checks, zero swap.

Lead review caveat: `heap : HeapMirror` is a free parameter, and the
extractor's allocation cost is read from it. `heap.bits = 0` satisfies
AllocationBound trivially, so the allocation part is not yet tied to the
real extractor's heap use. The report labels it the single residual and does
not claim M10 closed, which is correct, and the headline must not cite M10 as
discharged until `heap` is fixed to the actual allocation mirror. Pass 8:
define that mirror, where each stage's allocations are counted where they
happen, and prove bits ≤ (fixed width) × (proved work), since each allocated
element costs at least one unit of work. Then instantiate heap with it and
remove the premise.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 03:20): headline v3 — M10 fully discharged

Landed from `codex/cx-loglen-20261021`. `R1FinalSoundnessHeadlineV3.final_soundness`
removes `h_M10_log_length` with nothing added (the lead checked the signature
diff). The recorded log decomposes exactly as (prover ++ verifier) ++
verifier, so its length is ≤ Q + 1122 ≤ Q + 1161 using `hqueries`. κ, the
soundness range and the cubic cost bound are unchanged. Compile 14 s,
3.65 GB, zero swap, standard axioms. All M10 premises (ledger, uniform,
allocation, log length) are now discharged. Current headline soundness
endpoint: `R1FinalSoundnessHeadlineV3.final_soundness`. Direct premises
left: hqueries (the experiment's query budget), R16/R13/R14/R15/R3 on
collision-free runs, the tag rejection law, and the BoundaryEvidence fields

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-21 09:40): oracle-decision port ADOPTED and merged

The user approved the port and the release wording on 2026-10-21. The
wording: "100-bit classical security: any attacker making at most Q hash
queries forges with probability at most (Q+1)/2^100", machine-checked in the
random-oracle model, in the same units as Zcash's 126. Until the source
correspondence closes, it is qualified with "correspondence to the deployed
verifier in progress".

The draft stack (oracle port → join split → short circuit → inner order →
refine join → accepted-run log → frozen order → cone audit → checkpoint ABI)
is cherry-picked onto research (69 commits, ending at febcf99c3). The merge
gate passed (evidence `port-integrate-20261021/`): 119 port modules compiled
in dependency order on current research (909 s total, peak 3.69 GB, zero
swap); `OraclePortHeadlineFrozen.final_soundness` and `per_query` use only
propext/Classical.choice/Quot.sound; 366 new .lean files scan clean; and no
pre-existing Lean module, lean-candidate or shipped crate changed.

**New headline soundness endpoint: `OraclePortHeadlineFrozen.final_soundness`**
(`OraclePortFrozenOrderHeadline.lean`). Its premises are:
- `hqueries`, the adversary query budget;
- `AcceptedRunJoin D rust`, saying that on every run the frozen Rust verifier
  accepts, its oracle log and outputs equal those of dV_frozen, the
  modelled frozen verifier with the exact frozen multiproof order;
- `StoredCarrierBinding`, the runtime binding of the stored proof bytes.

R16, R13, R15, R3, R14's model part and the tag law are theorems at dV_frozen.
The bound is κ(Q+1161) + (Q+1080)·B + …, giving (Q+1)·2^-100 for 32 ≤ Q ≤ Qmax,
which is equivalent to max(Q+1, 33)·2^-100 for every Q. Extractor cost is at most
4·scale³·(Q+2323)³. `R1FinalSoundnessHeadlineV3` remains as the pure-decision
predecessor. Remaining work is the per-cone accepted-run joins in
`design/JOIN-SPLIT-DRAFT-20261021.md` and `design/CONE-AUDIT-20261021.md`
(checkpoint ABI, parser and multiproof in progress by the lead; S1/S3/S4/S5/S9/S10
with session A), plus the runtime carrier binding.

## Correction (lead, 2026-10-21 10:55): the adoption note misstated the target

The 09:40 adoption addendum gave the release criterion as "(Q+1)/2^100" and
omitted, in its opening, the theorem's 32 ≤ Q qualification. The binding
criterion is the user's 15 October target: 100-bit per-attempt soundness
without grinding credit, plus the plain query-dependent repeated-attempt
bound. It is now written down in `design/SECURITY-CONTRACT-20261021.md`. Against
it, the port endpoint does not yet meet the per-attempt clause at the
whole-experiment level (Q = 0 gives ≈ 2^-95 because of the conservative 1080
starts); V3 does (≈ 0.996·2^-100). A start-charging fix is in progress.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-22 00:35): checkpoint bytes, partial

Landed from `codex/cx-ckpt-bytes-20261021` (evidence `ckpt-bytes-20261021/STATUS.md`):

Two boundaries recorded as theorems, both stopping only an over-broad
formulation: (1) the standalone reference `decode_k_spec` has no length guard
and accepts a 17-byte input, so the strict-K claim must be made at the
exact-width production `Reader.k` section, which is now an explicit obligation
to verify, not assume; (2) `heap::filled` on resume can fail with
`Error::Allocation`, so the checkpoint join is stated success-conditioned
(accepted-run form), which is what `AcceptedRunJoin` needs.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-22 03:00): checkpoint decoders identified; ruling on allocation errors

Landed from `codex/cx-ckpt-bytes3-20261022` (evidence `ckpt-bytes3-20261022/`):
explicit source/reference namespace transport; kernel identification of the
extracted K/E decoders with C5 at exact widths 16/32, including rejection;
bounded reader refinement and fallible-allocation content, with five per-operation
Kani gates. 35 standard-axiom reports, zero swap, no shipped change.

**Lead ruling on the error scope.** The source resume can return
`Error::Allocation`, which `strictStage` does not represent. The soundness
interface is `AcceptedRunJoin`, which constrains accepted runs only. A run
that returns any error, including `Error::Allocation`, is a rejection, and
extra rejections cannot increase the soundness failure probability. So the
checkpoint stage join is stated success-conditioned: if the source resume
returns `Ok`, its outputs and the six digest reads equal `strictStage`'s;
error returns map to the model's rejection. This is the accepted-run form, not
a new premise, and it says nothing about liveness (allocation availability is
an availability question, outside the soundness contract).

Open: complete concrete `ResumeOps`, semantic/weight section refinements,
`successful_fields` consumption, and the success-conditioned stage theorem.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-23 01:20): S2 diagnosis and route B adopted (delegated decision, D177)

Diagnosis (`evidence/s2-lead-20261023/DIAGNOSIS.md`): the v7 counterexample shows
the proposed contract's *representation* is insufficient. Its postcondition sees
the borrow counter go 0→1 and passes the integer-address checks, but the typed
guard's Drop reads 0 and leaves the account counter at 1. Integer address equality
does not encode typed guard identity, provenance or borrow ownership. This is not
evidence of a shipped SDK bug. Plan: `design/S2-LEAD-PLAN-20261023.md` (nine work
packages, Required14 mapping).

**Decision (lead, under the user's overnight delegation D177; reversible by the
user):** adopt **route B**. Add one named trust item, `S2TrustedSdkBorrow`: the
pinned Solana SDK `AccountInfo` data-borrow semantics and the underlying `RefCell`
guard semantics, in exactly the fifteen fields of the plan
(`account_data_cell`, `cell_payload`, `cell_counter`, `shared_ok`, `shared_err`,
`exclusive_ok`, `exclusive_err`, `shared_deref`, `exclusive_deref`,
`guard_move`, `shared_drop`, `exclusive_drop`, `error_move_drop`,
`borrow_frame`, `guard_liveness`), pinned to solana-account-info 2.3.0 and the
production dependency lock once checked. Reasons: the same policy as TrustedStd
(D133/D145), which trusts documented library semantics rather than verifying library
internals; route A has repeatedly timed out on the SDK's guard internals; the
fields mention no caller success, bytes, output, log or interface field, per the plan's
conclusion-hiding audit. D157 excluded interior mutability from its standing
permission, so this is a distinct ruling. It is listed in the headline trust list
beside TrustedStd, P6/P7, E1 and E2. Excluded and still requiring proof or a further
ruling: `Ref::clone`/`map_split`/`leak`, realloc, Rc clone/free, lamport borrows,
CPI, re-entrancy and concurrency. Every caller obligation remains a proof
obligation.

Merged `codex/cx-s2{a,b,c,d}-20261023` (reports `design/S2A..S2D-20261023.md`); all
use standard axioms only, and no shipped file changed.
- **A:** borrow-admission inversion using only `S2TrustedSdkBorrow`; original-payload
  transport; decoder/encoder carrier joins; the separate validator premise
  removed; four native prefix windows covering all 688 bytes.
- **B:** conditional transport of the same 1,768-byte checkpoint into `tx1` with
  observer None; original suffix/split facts; error mapping; consistency of each
  child's ordered digest log; syscall value laws.
- **C:** symbolic copy regions; per-guard drops; ordered cleanup composition;
  conditional CP-byte, proof/public-frame, geometry, counter-restoration and metadata
  laws (ten isolated Kani targets, no whole-frame harness).
- **D:** error-site inventory; execution-indexed error-prefix and borrow-case laws;
  guard-drop safety; the verbatim release observer is `None` (Kani).
**Shared obstruction (AGENTS.md discipline §7):** all four prove laws *conditional on
cut observations of one execution E*, but nothing yet constructs E from the native
accepted run. Lead ruling: build that once, using the P6 route that worked for the
prover. Write an owned reference of the adapter caller (stage 1 lines 946–991,
stage 2 lines 994–1030), with SDK calls expressed through the `S2TrustedSdkBorrow`
specification and children as opaque calls with their proved contracts. Extract it
with Aeneas, and Kani-check native caller = owned reference per source step with the
verified SDK stub. Then E is the reference execution, and success inversion yields the
cuts that A–D consume. On `Box::new` OOM (D): the Solana runtime aborts the transaction,
and a failed transaction's account writes are rolled back by the runtime. That is a
runtime fact to state under the existing runtime premises (P2–P4), and it is checked
against the pinned runtime rather than assumed silently. The decoder-name collisions B
reports are the import-union issue now being fixed globally.

backward-acceptance law is proved; the source driver `Port1_Driver.rust` runs the actual

stage substitution composed. Finding `Port1_CarrierObstruction.raw_join_forces_rejection`: the
frozen2 model `Input` carries a logical proof function independent of the physical bytes.
An all-input join therefore cannot hold for a byte-level source: change the logical
proof and keep the bytes, and the model rejects while the source still accepts.
**Lead ruling.** The deployed adversary submits bytes; there is no other proof object. The
correct interface is a source-indexed canonical carrier, where the logical proof is the
parse of the submitted stored bytes. State the join over such inputs, and prove that the
headline experiment's inputs are of this form, or transport accepted experiments to it
(the `StoredCarrierBinding` boundary). This corrects the interface, not the adversary: the
adversary still chooses arbitrary bytes, and anything it could do with an independent logical
proof object is not available to it in the deployed system. Before/after of the join statement
must be shown.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26): native Vec/Box ruling (no new trust item)

NFrm_ found that no accepted trust item specifies native Vec/Box ownership or effects, and that the shipped Claims code builds buffers with `uninit` and raw pointer writes, while the candidate theorem uses filled/boxed values. Ruling:
- (a) Safe Vec/Box operations that appear in Aeneas-extracted code are interpreted by Aeneas's standard library model. That model is part of the existing AeneasExtraction trust (D188). This clarifies D188; it does not widen it.
- (b) `unsafe` code, including MaybeUninit/uninit and raw pointer writes, is NOT covered by any trust item. It must go through the P6 route: an owned safe reference, Kani proving shipped equals reference (Result, effects and buffer contents), and Aeneas extraction of the reference.
- (c) The allocator's returning behaviour stays under the existing Allocator item.

Rows L174/L177/L179 are relaunched on this basis. Running total since the ledger baseline: 23 must-prove rows removed (L089, L090, writer admission, L164, L091-L093, L100-L109, authenticated_S1, L158, L183, L184, L175, L172), counting the composition rows. Target not yet established.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26): L157 is false at the installed instance; headline currently vacuous for deployed code

RawK_NativeDecision.accepted_pair (checked, standard axioms) shows that on accepted runs the source's first oracle query is the semantic transcript initialisation: the 38-byte domain `UNPROVED:R1:PRIVATE-SALTED:SEMANTIC:P3` ++ [0] ++ le32(public length) ++ public bytes ++ c1_root, first byte 85. Frozen2's first logged query is a batch0 kind0 leaf `0x10 0x71 ...` (first byte 16), or the empty log after an early rejection. So L157 (strict accepted ordered-log equality between source and frozen2, for every H) is FALSE at the installed DrvC_ instance. It is reclassified C. Consequence: EndP_Headline / DrvC_Headline / Par_Headline take L157 as a hypothesis and are therefore vacuous for the deployed code until L157 is replaced by a correct bridge. The composition rows removed on that path (L158, L183, L184, L160 and the parameter rows) stay valid as steps, but they don't by themselves yield a deployed headline. AGENTS.md section 4 applies: reordering or omitting queries needs a proved transport. Owner decision requested on the bridge architecture; a scoping job (LogAlign_) is mapping the full source/model log alignment. Target not yet established.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26): L157 scope corrected; the real mismatch is the checkpoint public carrier

design/LOG-ALIGNMENT-20261026.md (LogA_, 12 checked facts) corrects the scope of the previous addendum. RawK_'s first-position obstruction holds against bare dV_frozen2, NOT against the headline's complete verifier OraclePortStoredExperimentC4.verifier D dV_frozen2, which runs all 33 sampler rows (semantic initialisation first) before the decision. In the inspected full schedule no reordering is needed. The concrete mismatch is this: source checkpoint write/resume hashes the 1880-byte ASF8 physical public statement, while frozen2 hashes the semantic public bytes (1455/1427), which also changes the checksum inputs. Strict L157 stays false at the installed instance, and the headline stays vacuous until the bridge exists. Options and estimates are in the report: A transport (20-40 targets, high variance); B frozen3 with separate physical and semantic public carriers, keeping the sampler and batch order (8-16 model targets plus the common source join; no verifier/prover byte or CU change); C changing checkpoint hashing to H(U) (verifier bytes change). Lead recommendation: B. Owner decision requested, because the headline model named in the security contract would move from frozen2 to frozen3 (same game, metric and target). A frozen3 prototype (F3_) is launched as an unadopted candidate in the meantime.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26): frozen3 bounds proved; raw-join form ruling

F3T_ (frozen3-head; 148 standard-axiom audits) proves frozen3's model bounds: Q+34 charged starts, Q+1161 distinct reads (the frozen2 constant, unchanged), Q+2322 issued calls, the failure bound and the extractor cost. The exact zero-query margin 2^-108 - 6*(1161^2+2965^2)/2^256 - 2^-126 - 2^-454 > 0 holds with the single account collision budget 2965. Ruling (lead, same pattern as authenticated_S1 / AuS1_): the frozen3 replacement for L157 is stated as "raw canonical join OR the charged collision event". A strict raw join cannot hold on collision worlds, and the collision event's probability is already inside the bound. The headline consumes the disjunction; the bound charges the collision branch. This restates the premise in its correct cryptographic form. It does not weaken the target or the failure event. Still open: composed non-vacuity, the disjunctive raw join at the installed DrvC_ instance, and the candidate headline. frozen3 remains UNADOPTED pending the owner decision.

L159 ruling. SCB_ proved that literal L159 ('the ENTIRE accepted runtime run equals annotatedWith D rust (canon P)') is impossible for a single faithfully recorded execution with a nonempty verifier log, because annotatedWith re-issues the verifier's chronological oracle suffix (the second annotation pass). Corrected form: (i) the physical RunView equals the first (physical) pass of the installed source program on the deployed two-transaction game; (ii) the annotated run equals the physical run followed by the known-cell replay, proved as an annotation transport theorem, not assumed as runtime trust. ## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26 16:40 UTC): cross-stream quiet window mechanism

Stream B's prover timing gate (candidate median under 15 s and at most 1.10x research; D169) and its L3 collector need a quiet host. Agreed mechanism: a marker file /home/dombarker/project-offloads/QUIET_WINDOW.json {"until": epoch} makes the reservation scripts of both streams refuse admission until that time; workers wait-and-retry (local-lean rule 8); windows are capped at 30 minutes. First window: 16:50-17:20 UTC today. The soundness scripts (S2X_/S2H_reservation.py) are patched (backups *.bak-lead-20261026b); the idempotent patch is /tmp/quiet-window-patch.py on the host. ## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26 17:00 UTC): quiet window, second attempt; systemd-run shim

The 16:50 window was broken at 16:52 by a soundness launch through S2W_lean_host.py, which does not use the reservation scripts patched earlier; the per-job launcher copies on the host number in the hundreds, so script-by-script patching is unreliable. Replacement mechanism: ~/.local/bin/systemd-run on the host (PATH export at the top of ~/.bashrc so sshd remote commands use it; backup ~/.bashrc.bak-lead-20261026) waits outside any scope while the marker is in force (at most 35 min), then execs /usr/bin/systemd-run; the window owner bypasses with QUIET_WINDOW_OWNER=1. Tested both ways. Second window agreed: 17:45-18:15 UTC. No cap or limit changed.

## Addendum (lead, 2026-10-26, evening): L166 finding and ruling; D188 fidelity note

BEf_ (batch source Result/effects) found a reached discrepancy on a REJECTED path of the batch body: the shipped Rust returns Err(Parse) where the retained Aeneas extraction tail becomes `fail panic`, reached by the native TX1/TX2 fixtures after 10 of 12 leaf hashes; split Kani checks and a local Lean inequality confirm it. No accepted-path divergence and no acceptance vulnerability: both sides reject. Ruling: (1) under the success-conditioned rule (AcceptedRunJoin constrains accepted runs; error kinds do not matter), L166 is restated success-conditioned: the installed batch bodies' Result and effects on the ACCEPTED path equal the shipped source, and on every rejected prefix both reject and the oracle reads issued before rejection are counted (RPx_ accounting); the exact error kind is irrelevant to the failure event. (2) D188 fidelity note: the Aeneas extraction is known to map at least one error-propagation site to a panic; this divergence is confined to non-Ok outcomes and does not touch any acceptance-guarded theorem, but it is recorded against the D188 trust item for the release audit, and Stream B should check the same site class in the prover extraction. Target not yet established.

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-26: replay-dry6 (RPz5_) merged; FINDING: the cached top mixes two PublicPreparation extraction cohorts

`codex/cx-replay-dry6-20261026` (04b2c6bf24) merged; 694 of 1,500 attempts
used (source-finding stop, not exhaustion); 3,341 green contexts; L187 open;
delta 0. The three RPz4 failures were a cache/source pairing error and now
compile unchanged on the coherent SInt2 producer pair. The genuine finding
(RPz5_TX1-FINDING): ExtractedTx1Model (bridge-tx1-e5c186ba3) was compiled
against the larger e5c186ba3 PublicPreparation producer, which declares
verifier.Phase, verifier.Accumulator, r0.Error and the tx1 closures, while
S1SemanticPhaseV1 (session A's S1 source) redeclares verifier.Phase as an
abbreviation of FirstCaller.Phase and typechecks only against the smaller
producer. No single unchanged producer source satisfies both, so the cached
Rc10 closure is NOT a coherent source closure: it was assembled from objects
compiled against two different extraction outputs. This is source
incoherence, not a mathematical counterexample and not an SDK defect; it
does not by itself invalidate any individual theorem, but the composed top
cannot be claimed as a single closure until it is repaired. Ruling:
replay-dry7 (RPz6_) determines the faithful producer from extraction
provenance against the frozen D188 pin, supplies a lead-owned replacement for

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-27: fixture-tx2q (JGt_) merged; quiet-window admission defect (lead error) fixed

to the host reservation scripts wrote `allowed:false` but exited 0, while the
memory-contention path exits 78. Wrappers that read only the exit code
therefore took the refusal as admission. The host systemd-run shim held the
scope. The journal shows JGt_TX2_009 started at 13:39:49 UTC, 13 s after
Stream B removed the marker (13:39:36), so the op223 timing gate was not
contaminated. Fix: the quiet block now exits 78 in all five copies (two in
Stream B directories, with Stream B's agreement); backups are
*.bak-qw-exit-20261027 and a test refusal returns 78. local-lean.txt rule 9
now requires both `allowed:true` and exit 0. The shim hold limit is restored
to 2100 s. Attempt 009's Lean result (exit 0, standard axioms) stands as
recorded, together with its admission record. Continuation JGv_ launched.

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-27: leaf-p6-3 (LPc_) merged; shared P6 method ruling

`codex/cx-leaf-p6-3-20261027` (08309c42b1) merged; no shipped change; no
premise removed; no whole leaf of the ten closed. Closed on exact domains:
- the verifier::echeck tail stage (native, lengths 0-63 and both k), with the
  Lean model tail induction and the 928/29 bound;
- the native 28-value canonical-producer stage;
- the model decoder-to-boundary canonicality composition, with no supplied
  canonicality premise.
Kani: 20 runs, 8 passed, 11 timed out, 1 compile failure. Lean: 51 of 60
passed. The final audit shows standard axioms only.

Shared obstruction (AGENTS.md section 7): across LPc_ and LPe_, 17 of 40 Kani
runs timed out, in two shapes. One is field arithmetic on arbitrary canonical
operands (Dot::push cells, powers7, the Q product). The other is whole-buffer
native loops (464/928-byte expand, the whole wide_view frame, the 0/58 late
decode classes, the whole checkpoint call).

Lead ruling, binding on every P6 job:
(1) Field operations are proved once as Kani function contracts against the
    reference (proof_for_contract, split per output coordinate or limb product
    if needed) and used through stub_verified in every caller.
(2) Loops are never verified whole-buffer. A Kani harness proves ONE iteration
    on an arbitrary pre-state satisfying the stated invariant (the echeck
    K013-016 pattern), and the induction over the iteration count is done in
    Lean on the model.
(3) No harness timeout increase, and no rerun of a timed-out shape.
(4) A contract or invariant precondition stronger than what every caller
    establishes is a finding, not a pass.
Continuation LPg_ launched for the ten leaves under this ruling. LPf_ already
carries (1) and (3), and the ruling applies to it as stated here.

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-27: leaf-p6-7 (LPg_) merged; Lean-discharged field stubs ruling

`codex/cx-leaf-p6-7-20261027` (c2b592e096) merged as 9005d26087; no shipped
change; no premise removed; all ten leaves OPEN. Kani: 30 runs, 21 passed, 6
timed out. Five canonical field-helper contracts passed (reduce-range,
scalar-add, Q-add, Q-sub, Q-half), as did the native expand iteration and
fixed-offset decode_k frames. Even an isolated 31-bit primitive product checked
against a 128-bit reference timed out (K028): multiplier equivalence is the
known weak case for SAT, and contract splitting does not remove it. Also:
K029 omitted the -Z stubbing flag (a harness error, no proof result), and
K023 wide_view failed both pass-through pointer-equality assertions. That is
open for diagnosis, with no shipped-bug claim.

Ruling (binding on P6 jobs; supersedes item (1) of the shared P6 method ruling
for TRANSLATED field operations): field multiplication is never proved in
Kani. The field operations are translated (emitted aspis_core.field.M31.mul,
CM31.mul, QM31.mul, QM31.mul_m31, mul_by_r, wide_exact.WideExact.mul), and
Lean already proves the emitted mul equal to the probe model
(JFv_RawExtractedSemanticCallOpsV20.generated_*_mul_eq_probe). In caller
harnesses, each such operation is stubbed (kani::stub) by a function that
returns kani::any() constrained by kani::assume to its natural-number
specification on canonical inputs. Each stub is paired with a named Lean
theorem: on that domain the emitted definition returns ok with exactly that
value. The pairing (stub, Lean theorem, domain) is recorded per stub. A stub
with no paired Lean theorem is an open obligation, not a pass. Callers must
establish the stub's domain. Opaque (untranslated) multiplication, if any, is
decomposed into 32x32 partial products combined by a Lean lemma. Continuation
LPh_ launched for the ten leaves.

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-27: extraction-assoc8 (XRp_) merged; sampler association counterexample (open finding)

`codex/cx-extraction-assoc8-20261027` (3cd1b3491a) merged; no shipped change;
no premise removed; A0-A8 OPEN. Finding: XRp_LowSamplerObstruction.
all_blocks_law_false refutes, for the selected emitted kit, the A2 sampler law
`forall block, XRn_Join_Release.sampler.low_base_p_digits block =
AspisR1Post25Sampler.sampler.low_base_p_digits block`. On the block ff ff ff 7f
followed by 28 zero bytes (little-endian P), both executions succeed
(XRp_SamplerNonvacuity.both_succeed) and differ. The emitted kit returns eight
zero digits and quotient P; every candidate quotient is at most 256.

Lead reading of the frozen source (crates/aspis-r1-cost-probe/src/sampler.rs:56):
the from_fn closure mutably borrows `quotient` and rewrites its limbs through
`iter_mut().rev()`, so the eight long divisions chain. On P this gives digits
0, 1, 0, ... and final quotient 0, which is consistent with the candidate
model and not with the emitted kit. Aeneas does not define core.array.from_fn:
it emits it as an axiom template in FunsExternal. The project supplies the
externals; TerminalArrayFromFnModelV4 threads the closure state but drops the
final closure state. The worker locates the lost write in the iter_mut/rev
write-back restoration.

Status: OPEN, not yet classified. If the defect is in an external model used
by the ADOPTED release extraction, D188's "emission is the semantics" does not
hold for this function as instantiated, and the trust inventory needs
correction (owner escalation). If it is only in the worker-assembled XRn_Join
kit, it is an artefact to repair. This is NOT a shipped-Rust bug claim and
NOT a defect in the adopted soundness proof, which uses the candidate model.
Focused diagnostic job SDX_ launched to classify it: native run on the block,
the exact dropping definition, and its provenance in the adopted extraction.

paths lead to UNDETERMINED definitions, and 678 import candidates are
uninspected. The Windows import collision is resolved with checked prefixed

**Correction (AGENTS.md section 2, accuracy):** association results proved
against this kit are statements about the kit, not about the emission, until
each one's dependency path avoids unfaithful and undetermined stand-ins.

  the XRn_Join kit". Its lineage appears among the paths to UNDETERMINED
  definitions, so it receives no emission credit until re-checked.

  ledger count does not change. The 13 opaque-leaf P6 bridges are unaffected:
  they relate frozen native Rust to the adopted cohort models, not to the kit.

**Kit-method ruling (binding on all association work):**
1. The emitted side of any association row is the raw release Funs body,
   verbatim (evidence/extraction-assoc2-20261027/XRj_L013/sources/
   XRj_generated/XRh_Release/Funs.lean, SHA256 f4b2d17c...). Edited emitted
   bodies are reverted, or proved to differ only by names.
2. A shipped function that has an emitted body may not have a hand-written
   stand-in. Use the emitted body; use an adopted cohort definition only
   through a proved association.
3. Only Rust std/core externals with no emitted body (library models) may be
   written by hand. Each needs fidelity evidence: a citation to the pinned
   Aeneas std library (trust row P7), or a proof against documented Rust
   semantics plus a native differential.
4. Association credit is granted only to theorems whose kernel dependency
   path is entirely on raw emitted bodies, CLOSED P6 leaves, proved adapters
   and library models with fidelity evidence.

Rulings, binding on all bridge lanes:

   no dependency on an open unit proceed in parallel; never hold a unit
   behind an unrelated open member. Use the whole budget.
2. Runtime syscalls (SolanaDigest::digest / sol_sha256 / hashv). The native
   bridge proves the function passes the exact byte slices to the syscall
   and returns its reply unchanged. The digest VALUE comes from the existing
   runtime trust row (the realization sha256 = H at the S2W extraction
   boundary). Never unwind SHA, and never claim a Kani model of the syscall.
3. Cohort authority. Operation slot numbers, names and cohort definitions
   follow the replayed closure of AspisR1.RPx_Deployed.deployed
   (evidence/replay-deployed2-20261027, evidence/replay-tops3-20261027) and
   the coherent SInt2 objects. Where an inventory disagrees (here op48/op49

Kani: all 50 runs used, 44 passed. Lean: 19 attempts; 23 roots, standard
axioms only. multiproof, keys, batch and TX2 U09 remain OPEN.

Discrepancy (NBJ_COPY_FINDING.md). Harness K048 asserts the slice-copy
prefix/zero-suffix law on an arbitrary 16-word input, and it FAILS. K050's
concrete trace is input[7]=1, input[15]=512, others 0, n=16, k=15. That input
is not strictly increasing, so a real node_count call rejects it at
well_formed before the copy; this is NOT a shipped-code counterexample. Yet a
native optimized fixture on the same input (R001) prints expected 512 /
actual 512 and passes. A Kani property failure that native execution does not
reproduce means one of three things:
- the harness asserts something different from the fixture;
- the fixture is not the same input;
- the pinned Kani's slice-copy model diverges from execution.
The third would bear on every P6 bridge, since a modelling divergence could
also hide a false PASS. Ruling: the continuation first settles this. It
generates Kani's concrete-playback unit test for K050 (enabling the unstable
playback flag explicitly), runs it natively under the frozen lock, and
reports whether native execution fails on exactly that trace. If native
passes and Kani fails, the Kani model divergence is a finding to the lead
before any further P6 credit. Continuation NBM_ launched.

## Lead addendum, 2026-10-28 01:50 UTC: Kani copy discrepancy classified (c); Kani-fidelity diagnostic launched

NBM_ (32e7a92334): the Kani-generated concrete-playback test for the K050
trace, copied verbatim, PASSES natively (NBM_R003; frozen lock, optimized),
while Kani's verification of the same harness reports the assertion
assert_eq!(level[k], if k<n {input[k]} else {0}) FAILING after
level[..n].copy_from_slice(&input[..n]). NBM_K001 regenerates the identical
trace. Classification (c): the pinned Kani 0.67.0 verification result
diverges from native execution on this harness. The minimal reproducer is
NBM_copy.rs.txt; it involves no digest, field stub, protocol loop or
cryptographic input. Per the 01:35 ruling, bridge credit on the batch lane is
stopped.

Lead observation: the harness carries #[kani::unwind(2)] while the copy is 16
words. If Kani lowers copy_from_slice/copy_nonoverlapping to a loop, an
unwind bound below the copy length leaves later cells unwritten; that matches
level[15] = 0 where 512 is expected. That would be a harness-configuration
error rather than a Kani model defect, but the absence of an
unwinding-assertion failure in the report leaves it unproved.

Why it matters beyond this lane: if a Kani model can lose copied data, a
harness asserting something FALSE about copied cells could pass. Every P6 and
native bridge relies on Kani passes. Diagnostic KDX_ launched:
1. mechanism: unwinding-assertion settings, a re-run with an adequate unwind
   bound (a changed shape, permitted), and the CBMC trace value of level[15];
2. EXPOSURE: every credited Kani PASS in the merged P6 leaves and bridges
   whose harness copies memory (copy_from_slice, copy_nonoverlapping, clone of
   arrays) under an unwind bound smaller than the copy length, or with
   unwinding assertions disabled, listed with a verdict.
Until KDX_ reports, no NEW Kani pass of that shape receives credit. Existing
credit stands pending its exposure list.

### Lead addendum, 2026-10-28: NBO_ and NBU_ merged; ruling (s) on universally quantified adapter slots; Kani-exhaustion stop rule

NBO_ (native-query3) proved the complete emitted TX1 query/accumulate/finish Result composition against the checked reference for one concrete dictionary. It also proved every-caller domain propagation (NBO_EveryCaller.ready) and the same-writer-prefix connection; 49 theorems, standard axioms. The whole native composite stays OPEN: native dictionary/effect bindings at the source call sites, and the four_dots/fold/Horner result composition (op20/op17/op16). Copy-dependent passes were run before KMX_ and carry no copy credit.

NBU_ (native-tx2j) re-established the reached copy windows under the KMX_ stubs, with paired failing controls. It promoted the four U03 codecs, the ASF8 validator and encoder, and the two U04 helpers to native CLOSED-CONDITIONAL, conditional only on the named lower DigestEncode/DigestDecode/TransferEncode/WithdrawalEncode bridges. ASR8 (out of soundness scope) has one unpaired pass and no credit.

Ruling (s), on NBU_U04_SLOTS.md: the classification is accepted in a NARROWED form. Slots op15, op23, op25-34, op48 and op49 are fields of `missing`, which RPx_Deployed.deployed universally quantifies. But the headline also takes the premise `extraction : AeneasExtraction (sdk (config missing ...)) native1 native2 hashv`. Using the headline for the deployed program therefore requires exhibiting ONE `missing` for which that premise holds of the real native program. Universality removes the BODY-FIDELITY obligation: no model of what a registry or account decoder should compute is needed, because soundness holds whatever these ops return. It does NOT remove the obligation that the native behaviour is an instance. Per slot, the remaining obligation is:
(i) representability: the native callee's observable result (Result value, error, divergence) is a function of exactly the arguments the SDK passes to opN, with no other reads that affect it and no writes outside what the op returns (frame);
(ii) call-site association: the native callers invoke it at the SDK's opN call points, with the SAME arguments, and use only its result, so that missing.adapter.opN := (the native function) satisfies the extraction premise at those calls.
Then the member row is CLOSED as "instantiated by the native function", not as a body bridge. op53's earlier ruling is read the same way: emission correctness is not needed, but its call-site and frame instance is part of discharging `extraction` at the final audit. The 'not a soundness obligation' wording in NBU_U04_SLOTS.md is superseded by this ruling.

Stop rule (pattern again: NBO_ stopped at 40/40 Kani with 43/100 Lean, NBU_ at 50/50 Kani with 7/120 Lean): Kani exhaustion is NOT a stop while Lean-only obligations remain. A worker continues Lean-only work, then lists, member by member, why each remaining obligation needs Kani.

Continuations: NBW_ (native-query4, TX1 query composite) and NBX_ (native-tx2k, U04 slot instances under ruling (s), ASR8 control, U05 remainder).

### Lead addendum, 2026-10-28: NCU_ merged (checkpoint caller domain: reachability gap closed)

NCU_ (native-drvdom2, Lean only) proved for the model driver, with observer none, that every reached writer call shares the same preparation/query/flush prefix. ### Lead addendum, 2026-10-28 evening: NCY_ merged (release route); SBF stack-offset investigation

NCY_ (native-elfpins) reproduced the devnet verifier (63ad3e9c..., 28.5 s) and Pool (121f3e29..., 29.3 s) ELFs bit-exactly; all 1,243 input hashes matched before and after. With NCR_'s default (f8c0ec72...), all three deployed ELFs are now freshly reproduced from frozen source with the pinned recipe. NCY_ also wrote the deployment-row status and the single release trust-inventory draft (NCY_DEPLOYMENT_ROWS.md, NCY_TRUST_INVENTORY_DRAFT.md).

Possible soundness-relevant finding, under investigation: the SBF compiler prints stack-offset "Error:" diagnostics for 34 functions, although the builds exit 0. They include candidates on the deployed path: r1_cost_probe r1_wire/semantic_wire Prefix::point_claims, aspis_statement semantic_handoff and verify_semantics, core::array::try_from_fn and Vec::extend_trusted. Whether an over-limit frame traps (an access violation, so a safe rejection) or silently overwrites an adjacent frame depends on the deployed runtime's stack-frame model. Silent overwriting would be a concrete failure of the trusted P7 compiler-semantics row. NDC_ (native-sbfstack) determines reachability per ELF, actual frame sizes and the runtime behaviour from the pinned rbpf source. It builds a local demonstration only if corruption is possible, never touching deployed programs or keys. Any change to the deployed program stays with the owner.

### Lead addendum, 2026-10-28 evening: NCZ_ composition ledger; NDA_ merged; frontier lane 1

NCZ_ (native-compledger) built the validated composition ledger (evidence/native-compledger-20261028/NCZ_LEDGER.json, NCZ_validate.py; 14 tests pass; 7,363 artifact pins validate). It has 587 reached rows over 434 distinct functions: 213 locally CLOSED-CONDITIONAL, 370 OPEN, and 4 quarantined occurrences; composed status stays OPEN everywhere. A minimum reachability cover of the OPEN functions has 133 representatives. The top ones are small arithmetic and helper functions (M31::reduce_u62 with 40 dependants, PreparedQm31Multiplier::new 34, weights.rs::field 31, CM31::from_m31 and qm31_pack_base4 29). These are exactly the shape where the full medium (reference, extraction, Lean equality, Kani contract) works. Frontier lane 1, NDD_ (native-front1), takes the field/wide_exact/weights/multiproof representatives.

NDA_ (native-drvdom3, Lean only) proved S2, all 22 query bounds with distinctness, and canonical gamma, kappa, alpha, rho, semanticAlpha and claim, on the same model prefix. The checkpoint leaf retains only three caller groups (four z-coordinates, rhs and power canonicality), and NDA_DriverDomain.domain_iff shows these equivalent to the full writer domain on that prefix. Its stop at 21/50 Lean is justified member by member: the missing primitive bindings belong to the query/finish lanes.
